be a proper minor-closed graph class and let
h\coloneqq h(\mathcal{H})
the largest size of an obstruction of
\mathcal{H}.

There exists a function
f_{\ref{lemma_no_Hlinked_sets}}\colon\mathbb{N}^{2}\to\mathbb{N}
depending only on
h
such that, for every graph
G
and every positive integer
k
one of the following statements holds

1.

there exists
\mathscr{W}=\langle\mathscr{W}_{t}\mid t\in\mathbb{N}\rangle\in\mathfrak{W}_{\mathcal{H}}
such that
G
contains a subdivision of
\mathscr{W}_{k},
or

2.

\mathcal{H}\text{-}\mathsf{link}(G)\leq f_{\ref{lemma_no_Hlinked_sets}}(h,k).

Proof.

Let
c
be the constant from
Theorem 11.10
.
We set
f_{\ref{lemma_no_Hlinked_sets}}(h,k)\coloneqq 36c\cdot f^{1}_{\ref{thm_local_structure}}(h,k)^{20}+3.

Now suppose
G
contains an
f_{\ref{lemma_no_Hlinked_sets}}(h,k)
-
\mathcal{H}
-linked set
X.

Then, in particular, for every set
S\subseteq V(G)
of order at most
f_{\ref{lemma_no_Hlinked_sets}}(h,k)
there exists a unique component
J_{X,S}
such that
|V(J_{X,S})\cap X|>\frac{2}{3}|X|.

Hence,
X
is also
f_{\ref{lemma_no_Hlinked_sets}}(h,k)
-well-linked.

It follows from
Theorem 11.10
that there exists a
f^{1}_{\ref{thm_local_structure}}(h,k)
-wall
W
which agrees with
X.

Now, by
Theorem 9.2
one of to things hold.
Either

1.

G
contains
K_{3k^{2}+kh}
as a minor, or

2.

there exists a set
A\subseteq V(G)
of size at most
f^{2}_{\ref{thm_local_structure}}(h,k)<f^{1}_{\ref{thm_local_structure}}(h,k)<f_{\ref{lemma_no_Hlinked_sets}}(h,k)
and a set
\mathcal{Z}
of graphs of size at most
h
such that the component
J_{X,A}
is
Z
-minor-free for all graphs on at most
h
vertices which do not belong to
\mathcal{Z}.

In the first case,
G
must contain, as a minor, the walloid of order
k
which has
k
copies of
K_{h}
in a single flower.
Clearly, this walloid contains a member of
\mathfrak{W}_{\mathcal{H}}
of order
k
as desired.

Hence, we may assume we are in the second outcome.
Since
J_{X,A}\notin\mathcal{H}
and every obstruction of
\mathcal{H}
has size at most
h,
there must exist some graph
H\in\mathsf{obs}(\mathcal{H})\cap\mathcal{Z}.

Therefore, the second outcome of
Theorem 9.2
holds for
H
which means that there exists
\mathscr{W}^{H}=\langle\mathscr{W}^{H}_{t}\mid t\in\mathbb{N}\rangle\in\mathfrak{W}_{\mathsf{excl}(H)}
such that
G
contains a subdivision
W_{H}
of
\mathscr{W}^{H}_{k}.

Moreover, the base cylinder of
W_{H}
is a subgraph of
W.

Since
\mathfrak{W}_{\mathsf{excl}(H)}\subseteq\mathfrak{W}_{\mathcal{H}}
this is the first outcome of our claim.
Hence, if the first outcome does not hold it must be true that
\mathcal{H}\text{-}\mathsf{link}(G)\leq f_{\ref{lemma_no_Hlinked_sets}}(h,k).

∎

Please notice that, with standard techniques for finding balanced separators,
Theorem 11.10
, and
Theorem 9.2
the procedure described in the proof of
Lemma 11.5
yields an algorithm that either finds one of the obstructions, and therefore a large half-integral
\mathcal{H}
-barrier, or an
\mathcal{H}
-tree decomposition of bounded width in time
\mathcal{O}_{h,k}(|G|^{4}\log|G|).

Based on this decomposition we can then proceed as discussed in the proof of
Lemma 12.3
to either find a bounded size modulator to
\mathcal{H}
or a large integral
\mathcal{H}
-barrier.
The purpose of
Section 12
is to make this more explicit.

We are now ready to give a proof of
Theorem 1.5
.

Proof of
Theorem 1.5
.

Let
f_{\ref{main_grid_general}}(h,k)\coloneqq 4\cdot f_{\ref{lemma_no_Hlinked_sets}}(h,k)+8.

By
Corollary 11.6
it follows that, if
\mathcal{H}\text{-}\mathsf{tw}(G)\geq f_{\ref{main_grid_general}}(h,k),
then
\mathcal{H}\text{-}\mathsf{link}(G)\geq f_{\ref{lemma_no_Hlinked_sets}}(h,k)+1.

This means that the second outcome of
Lemma 11.11
is impossible and thus, the first outcome must hold as desired.
∎

12
Universal obstructions for
{\sf apex}_{\mathcal{H}}
and
\mathcal{H}
-
td

In this section, we give a proof that
\mathfrak{F}_{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathsf{min}}
is a universal obstruction for
{\sf apex}_{\mathcal{H}}.

For this, we proceed in two steps. First, in
Subsection 12.1
, we prove this result
for the case where all obstructions of
\mathcal{H}

are connected, which is equivalent to the property
that
\mathcal{H}
is closed under taking disjoint subgraphs.
Then, using this special case as a starting point, we prove the general result in
Subsection 12.2
.
In
Subsection 12.3
we apply similar ideas as those of
Subsection 12.2
in order to find an obstruction set for the parameter of
elimination distance
to
\mathcal{H},
defined by
Bulian and Dawar in
[
9
]
.

The parameter
{\sf apex}_{\mathcal{H}}
has been extensively studied
for different instantiations of
\mathcal{H}.
Given a proper minor-closed
class
\mathcal{H}
we define, for every
k\in\mathbb{N},

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\mathcal{P}_{k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{G\mid{\sf apex}_{\mathcal{H}}(G)\leq k\}

(21)

In
[
2
]
it was shown that for every
\mathcal{H}

there is a computable
function
f^{\sf ap}_{\mathcal{H}}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}

such that for every
k\in\mathbb{N},
if
Z\in{\sf obs}(\mathcal{A}\mathcal{P}_{k}),
then
|Z|\leq f^{\sf ap}_{\mathcal{H}}(k)
and this implied that the problem of checking whether
{\sf apex}_{\mathcal{H}}(G)\leq k
is constructively in
FPT
when parameterized by
k
(see also
[
22
]
).
Recently, in
[
73
]
an explicit bound of
f^{\sf ap}_{\mathcal{H}}(k)=2^{2^{2^{2^{\text{$\mathsf{poly}$}_{h}(k)}}}}
was given.
Also, in
[
72
]
, an algorithm checking whether
{\sf apex}_{\mathcal{H}}(G)\leq k
was given, running in time
2^{\text{$\mathsf{poly}$}_{h}(k)}|G|^{2}.

12.1
Global apex pairs

Given a graph
Z
we define the
connectivization
of
Z,
denoted by
\mathsf{conn}(Z),
as the set of all edge-minimal connected graphs that contains
Z
as a subgraph.
Notice that every graph
\mathsf{conn}(Z)
is the graph
Z
plus a minimal number of edges that make it connected. Therefore,
|\mathsf{conn}(Z)|=2^{\mathcal{O}(|Z|\cdot\log|Z|)}.

Given a finite set
\mathcal{Z}
of graphs, we define its
minor-connectivization
as the set
\mathsf{conn}(\mathcal{Z})=\mathsf{min}(\{\mathsf{conn}(Z)\mid Z\in\mathcal{Z}\})
where, given some set of graphs
\mathcal{A},
we use
\mathsf{min}(\mathcal{A})
for the set of minor-minimal graphs in
\mathcal{A}.

Given a proper minor-closed class
\mathcal{H}
we define its
connectivity closure
to be

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{(c)}\coloneqq\{G\mid\text{every connected component of }G\text{ belongs to }\mathcal{H}\}.

The obstructions for
\mathcal{H}^{(c)}
were described by Bulian and Dawar
[
10
]
as follows.

Proposition 12.1
.

For every proper minor-closed class
\mathcal{H}
it holds that
{\sf obs}(\mathcal{H}^{(c)})=\mathsf{conn}({\sf obs}(\mathcal{H})).

We define the parameter
{\sf size}\colon\mathcal{G}_{{\text{\rm{all}}}}\to\mathbb{N}
such that
{\sf size}(G)=|G|.

Based on this, by replacing
{\sf tw}
with size
{\sf size}
in (
6
) we define the following parameter:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf size}(G)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\min\{k\mid\text{there exists }X\subseteq V(G)\text{ s.{}t.{}\penalty\ }|X|\leq k\text{\penalty\ and\penalty\ }\text{$G-X\in\mathcal{H}^{(c)}$}\}

Observe that
\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf size}={\sf apex}_{\mathcal{H}^{(c)}}
if and only if all obstructions of
\mathcal{H}
are connected.
However, in general
\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf size}\preceq{\sf apex}_{\mathcal{H}}.

Let

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq

\displaystyle\mathfrak{W}_{\mathcal{H}}\cup\mathfrak{W}_{\mathcal{H}^{(c)}}^{\varnothing}

(22)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}_{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathsf{min}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq

\displaystyle\mathsf{min}(\mathfrak{S}_{\mathcal{H}}).

Next, we prove that
\mathfrak{S}_{\mathcal{H}}
is an obstructing set for
\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf size}.

We first prove the lower bound.

Observation 12.2
.

For every minor-closed class
\mathcal{H},
it holds that
{\sf p}_{\mathfrak{S}_{\mathcal{H}}}\preceq\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf size}
with linear gap function.

Proof.

Recall that
{\sf p}_{\mathfrak{S}_{\mathcal{H}}}=\max\{{\sf p}_{\mathfrak{W}_{\mathcal{H}}},{\sf p}_{\mathfrak{W}^{\varnothing}_{\mathcal{H}^{(c)}}}\}.

The inequality
{\sf p}_{\mathfrak{W}_{\mathcal{H}^{(c)}}^{\varnothing}}\preceq\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf size},
with linear gap function, follows from
Proposition 12.1
and the fact that for every
Z\in\mathsf{conn}({\sf obs}(\mathcal{H})),

\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf size}(k\cdot Z)=k.

The other inequality
{\sf p}_{\mathfrak{W}_{\mathcal{H}}}\preceq\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf tw},
with linear gap function, holds because of
Corollary 11.8
and
\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf tw}\leq\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf size}.

∎

Now we prove the upper bound, i.e., the direction
\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf size}\preceq{\sf p}_{\mathfrak{S}_{\mathcal{H}}}.

Lemma 12.3
.

There exists a function
f_{\ref{obs_con_apex}}\colon\mathbb{N}^{2}\to\mathbb{N}
such that for proper every minor-closed class
\mathcal{H},
where
h(\mathcal{H})=h,
every graph
G,
and every
k\in\mathbb{N},
either
{\sf p}_{\mathfrak{S}_{\mathcal{H}}}(G)\geq k
or
\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf size}(G)\leq f_{\ref{obs_con_apex}}(h,k),
where
f_{\ref{obs_con_apex}}(h,k)={2^{k^{\mathcal{O}_{h}(1)}}}.

Moreover, there exists an algorithm that, given
{\sf obs}(\mathcal{H}),

G,
and
k,
outputs a certificate of one of the two
outcomes in time
2^{2^{k^{\mathcal{O}_{h}(1)}}}|G|^{4}\log|G|.

Proof.

Let
G
be a graph where
{\sf p}_{\mathfrak{S}_{\mathcal{H}}}(G)\leq k.

This implies that
{\sf p}_{\mathfrak{W}_{\mathcal{H}}}(G)\leq{\sf p}_{\mathfrak{S}_{\mathcal{H}}}(G)\leq k
and
{\sf p}_{\mathfrak{W}_{\mathcal{H}^{(c)}}^{\varnothing}}(G)\leq{\sf p}_{\mathfrak{S}_{\mathcal{H}}}(G)\leq k.

As
{\sf p}_{\mathfrak{W}_{\mathcal{H}}}(G)\leq k,
due to
Theorem 1.5
, we have that
\mathcal{H}
-
{\sf tw}(G)\leq f_{\ref{main_grid_general}}(h,k).

Let
q\coloneqq f_{\ref{main_grid_general}}(h,k)
and let
(T,\beta)
be an
\mathcal{H}
-tree decomposition
of
G
of width at most
q.

Let
\mathsf{mp}(G)
be the maximum size of a collection
\mathcal{C}
of pairwise disjoint subgraphs of
G
such that for every
C\in\mathcal{C}
there is some graph in
\mathsf{conn}({\sf obs}(\mathcal{H}))
which is a minor of
C.

Observe that
\mathsf{mp}(G)\leq|\mathsf{conn}({\sf obs}(\mathcal{H}))|\cdot k,
otherwise
G
would contain a
\mathcal{H}^{(c)}
-barrier of size larger than
k
which would contradict the fact that
{\sf p}_{\mathfrak{W}_{\mathcal{H}^{(c)}}^{\varnothing}}(G)\leq k.

Let
p
be some positive integer.
We claim that if
\mathsf{mp}(G)\in[p]
then
\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf size}(G)\leq|S|\leq(q+1)\cdot(2p-1).

We prove this by induction on
p.

Given an edge
tt^{\prime}
of
T
where
T_{1}
(resp.
T_{2}
) is the connected component of
T-e
containing
t
(resp.
t^{\prime}
), we define

\displaystyle X_{t}\coloneqq\bigcup_{b\in V(T_{1})\setminus\{t\}}\beta(b)

and

\displaystyle X_{t^{\prime}}\coloneqq\bigcup_{b\in V(T_{2})\setminus\{t^{\prime}\}}\beta(b)

(23)

If, for some edge
tt^{\prime}\in E(T),
it holds that
\mathsf{mp}(G[X_{t}\setminus X_{t^{\prime}}])=p_{1}\geq 1
and
\mathsf{mp}(G[X_{t^{\prime}}\setminus X_{t}])=p_{2}\geq 1,
then we have that
p_{1}+p_{1}\leq p

and, from the induction hypothesis it follows that
\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf size}(G)\leq|X_{t}\cap X_{t^{\prime}}|+\mathsf{mp}(G[X_{t}\setminus X_{t^{\prime}}])+\mathsf{mp}(G[X_{t^{\prime}}\setminus X_{t}])\leq(q+1)+(q+1)\cdot(2p_{1}-1)+(q+1)\cdot(2p_{2}-1)\leq(q+1)\cdot(2p-1).

If there exists some edge
tt^{\prime}\in E(G)
such that
\mathsf{mp}(G[X_{t}\setminus X_{t^{\prime}}])=p_{1}=0
and
\mathsf{mp}(G[X_{t^{\prime}}\setminus X_{t}])=p_{2}=0,
we obtain that every component of
G-(X_{t}\cap X_{t^{\prime}})
belongs to
\mathcal{H}
which would complete our proof.

The only remaining case is where for every edge
tt^{\prime}
of
T,
either
\mathsf{mp}(G[X_{t}\setminus X_{t^{\prime}}])=p_{1}=0
or
\mathsf{mp}(G[X_{t^{\prime}}\setminus X_{t}])=p_{2}=0,
but not both.

In this case, we orient each
tt^{\prime}
towards the component where the value of
\mathsf{mp}
is positive.
This means that there is a node
t
of
T
which is a sink of the resulting orientation of
T.

Thus,
G-X_{t}
does not contain any graph of
\mathsf{conn}({\sf obs}(\mathcal{H}))
as a minor.
Therefore, every component of
G-X_{t}
belongs to
\mathcal{H}
and
X_{t}
is an
\mathcal{H}^{(c)}
-modulator of
G
of size
q+1\leq(q+1)\cdot(2p-1)
as desired.

Applying the claim above for
p=|\mathsf{conn}({\sf obs}(\mathcal{H}))|\cdot k,
one may obtains that

\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf size}(G)\leq 2(q+1)\cdot|\mathsf{conn}({\sf obs}(\mathcal{H}))|\cdot k.

Recall that
|\mathsf{conn}({\sf obs}(\mathcal{H}))|=2^{\mathcal{O}(h^{2})}.

Therefore the lemma holds for some
f_{\ref{obs_con_apex}}(h,k)={2^{k^{\mathcal{O}_{h}(1)}}}.

∎

We just proved that
{\sf p}_{\mathfrak{S}_{\mathcal{H}}}\sim\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf size}
with a single-exponential gap. This is a first step towards the main result of this section, which is that
\mathfrak{F}_{\mathcal{H}}
is an obstructing set for
{\sf apex}_{\mathcal{H}}.

This is proved in the next subsection.

12.2
The disconnected case

Given a graph
Z
we use
\mathsf{cc}(Z)
for the set of its components.
Given two graphs
Z_{1}
and
Z_{2},
we denote by
Z_{1}+Z_{2}
the disjoint union of
Z_{1}
and
Z_{2}.

Also, given two parametric graphs
\mathscr{G}^{i}=\langle\mathscr{G}^{i}_{t}\mid t\in\mathbb{N}\rangle,i\in[2],
we write
\mathscr{G}^{1}+\mathscr{G}^{2}\coloneqq\langle\mathscr{G}^{1}_{t}+\mathscr{G}^{2}_{t}\mid t\in\mathbb{N}\rangle
for their index-wise disjoint union.
Moreover, if
\mathfrak{G}^{i},i\in[2]
are families of parametric graphs, we define the parametric family

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}^{1}\otimes\mathfrak{G}^{2}\coloneqq\{\mathscr{G}^{1}+\mathscr{G}^{2}\mid(\mathscr{G}^{1},\mathscr{G}^{2})\in\mathfrak{G}^{1}\times\mathfrak{G}^{2}\}.

Notice that the compositions defined above are commutative and they may be extended in the natural way to
Z_{1}+\cdots+Z_{r},
to
\mathscr{G}^{1}+\cdots+\mathscr{G}^{r}
and to

\mathfrak{G}^{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathfrak{G}^{r}
respectively.

Let
\mathcal{H}
be a minor-closed class and let
Z\in{\sf obs}(\mathcal{H}).

Observe that
\mathfrak{F}^{(Z)}=\mathfrak{F}_{{\sf excl}(Z)}.

Given the notation above, the definitions of
\mathfrak{F}_{\mathcal{H}}
and its related families in
\eqref{first_walloid_formula},

\eqref{second_walloid_formula},
and
(
11
)
can be rewritten as follows.

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}_{{\sf excl}(Z)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}_{{\sf excl}(Z_{1})}\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathfrak{F}_{{\sf excl}(Z_{r})},\text{\penalty\ where $\mathsf{cc}(Z)=\{Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{r}\}$}

(24)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}_{\mathcal{H}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq

\displaystyle\big\{\mathscr{W}\in{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}\{\mathfrak{F}_{{\sf excl}(Z)}\mid{Z\in{\sf obs}(\mathcal{H})}\}\mid\text{for every $\mathscr{W}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{F}_{\mathcal{H}}^{\varnothing}$ it holds that $\mathscr{W}^{\prime}\not\lesssim\mathscr{W}$}\big\}

(25)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{F}}_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}_{\mathcal{H}}\cup\mathfrak{F}_{\mathcal{H}}^{\varnothing}

(26)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}_{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathsf{min}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq

\displaystyle\mathsf{min}(\mathfrak{F}_{\mathcal{H}})

(27)

Observation 12.4
.

Let
G^{1},G^{2},

F^{1},
and
F^{2}
be graphs, then

\displaystyle{\sf barrier}_{{\sf excl}(F^{1}+F^{2})}(G_{1}+G_{2})\geq\max\big\{{\sf barrier}_{{\sf excl}(F^{1})}(G_{1}),{\sf barrier}_{{\sf excl}(F^{2})}(G_{2}\big\}.

Lemma 12.5
.

Let
Z_{1}
and
Z_{2}
be two graphs and let
\mathfrak{F}^{1}
and
\mathfrak{F}^{2}
be obstructing sets for
{\sf apex}_{{\sf excl}(Z_{1})}
and
{\sf apex}_{{\sf excl}(Z_{2})}
respectively both with gap function
f.

Then
{\sf p}_{\mathfrak{F}^{1}\otimes\mathfrak{F}^{2}}\preceq{\sf apex}_{{\sf excl}(Z_{1}+Z_{2})}.

Proof.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\mathscr{F}_{k}^{i}\in\mathscr{F}^{i}\in\mathfrak{F}^{i},i\in[2].

If a graph
G
contains some graph
\mathscr{F}_{k}^{1}+\mathscr{F}_{k}^{2}
as a minor, then
{\sf apex}_{\mathcal{H}}(G)\geq{\sf apex}_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathscr{F}_{k}^{1}+\mathscr{F}_{k}^{2})\geq{\sf barrier}_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathscr{F}_{k}^{1}+\mathscr{F}_{k}^{2}),
which, by
Observation 12.4
, is at least
\max\{{\sf barrier}_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}(\mathscr{F}_{k}^{1}),{\sf barrier}_{\mathcal{H}^{2}}(\mathscr{F}_{k}^{2})\}\geq f(k).

This immediately implies that
{\sf p}_{\mathfrak{F}^{1}\otimes\mathfrak{F}^{2}}\preceq{\sf apex}_{{\sf excl}(Z_{1}+Z_{2})}.

∎

For every proper minor-closed class
\mathcal{H},
we define the parameter
{\sf p}^{\mathcal{H}}\coloneqq\max\{{\sf apex}_{{\sf excl}(Z)}\mid Z\in{\sf obs}(\mathcal{H})\}.

One may immediately observe the following.

Observation 12.6
.

For every proper minor-closed class
\mathcal{H},
it holds that
{\sf p}^{\mathcal{H}}\preceq{\sf apex}_{\mathcal{H}}\preceq|{\sf obs}(\mathcal{H})|\cdot{\sf p}^{\mathcal{H}}.

An immediate consequence of this observation is, that the parametric family
\mathfrak{F}_{\mathsf{excl}(Z)}
indeed defines a parameter that acts as a lower bound to
\mathsf{apex}_{\mathsf{excl}(Z)}
even if
Z
is not connected.

Corollary 12.7
.

For every proper minor-closed class
\mathcal{H},
it holds that
{\sf p}_{\mathfrak{F}_{\mathcal{H}}}\preceq{\sf apex}_{\mathcal{H}},
with linear gap.

Proof.

By applying
Lemma 12.5
inductively to (
24
), while using
Observation 12.2
as the base case, we obtain that for every graph
Z,

{\sf p}_{\mathfrak{F}_{{\sf excl}(Z)}}\preceq{\sf apex}_{{\sf excl}(Z)}.

The corollary holds due to the first inequality of
Observation 12.6
and the fact that
{\sf p}_{\mathfrak{F}_{\mathcal{H}}}=\max\{{\sf p}_{\mathfrak{F}_{{\sf excl}(Z)}}\mid Z\in{\sf obs}(\mathcal{H})\}.

∎

The next lemma is an algorithmic restatement of
Theorem 1.8
and we conclude this subsection with its proof.

Lemma 12.8
.

There exists a function
f_{\ref{apex_disc_obs}}\colon\mathbb{N}^{2}\to\mathbb{N}
such that for every proper minor-closed graph class
\mathcal{H},
where
h=h(\mathcal{H}),
every graph
G,
and every
k\in\mathbb{N},
either
{\sf p}_{\mathfrak{F}_{\mathcal{H}}}(G)\geq k
or
{\sf apex}_{\mathcal{H}}(G)\leq f_{\ref{apex_disc_obs}}(h,k),
where
f_{\ref{apex_disc_obs}}(h,k)={2^{k^{\mathcal{O}_{h}(1)}}}.

Moreover, there exists an algorithm that, given
{\sf obs}(\mathcal{H}),

G,
and
k,
outputs a certificate of one of the two outcomes in time
2^{2^{k^{\mathcal{O}_{h}(1)}}}|G|^{4}\log|G|.

Proof.

It suffices to prove that
{\sf apex}_{\mathcal{H}}\preceq{\sf p}_{\mathfrak{F}_{\mathcal{H}}}
since the other direction is already given by
Corollary 12.7
.

Let
G
be a graph.
As all graphs in
{\sf obs}(\mathcal{H})
have at most
h
vertices, we obtain that
|{\sf obs}(\mathcal{H})|=2^{\mathcal{O}(h^{2})}.

Due to the second inequality of
Observation 12.6
, we have that
{\sf apex}_{\mathcal{H}}(G)=2^{\mathcal{O}(h^{2})}\cdot{\sf p}^{\mathcal{H}}(G).

This justifies us to focus on a single graph rather than the entire set
\mathsf{obs}(\mathcal{H}).

Consider a graph
Z\in\mathsf{obs}(\mathcal{H}).

In this context, we fix
\mathsf{cc}(Z)=\{Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{r}\},

r\leq h.

Given a set
I=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{s}\}\subseteq[r]
of integers, we write
Z_{I}\coloneqq\bigcup_{i\in I}Z_{i}
and we write
\mathfrak{F}_{I}=\mathfrak{F}_{{\sf excl}(Z_{i_{1}})}\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathfrak{F}_{{\sf excl}(Z_{i_{s}})}
for those parametric graphs generated from the selection of components of
Z
induced by
I.

Given some integer
p\in[r],
a
p
-connectivization
of
Z
is a graph
Z^{\prime}
obtained from
Z
by selecting a set
I^{\prime}\subseteq[r]
with
|I^{\prime}|=p
and replacing the components
Z_{i},

i\in I^{\prime}
by a graph from
\mathsf{conn}(\sum_{i\in I^{\prime}}Z_{i}).

We denote by
\mathcal{Z}_{p}
the collection of all
p
-connectivizations of
Z.

Observe that, by definition of
\mathfrak{F}_{{\sf excl}(Z)},
it follows that for every
Z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}_{2},

\mathfrak{F}_{{\sf excl}(Z)}\lesssim\mathfrak{F}_{{\sf excl}(Z^{\prime})}.

Moreover there exists a function
c\coloneqq c(h)
such that for every
Z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}_{2},

\langle\mathscr{F}^{\prime}_{t}\rangle_{t\in\mathbb{N}}\in\mathfrak{F}_{{\sf excl}(Z^{\prime})}
there exists a
\langle\mathscr{F}_{t}\rangle_{t\in\mathbb{N}}\in\mathfrak{F}_{{\sf excl}(Z)}
such that
\mathscr{F}_{k}
is a minor of
\mathscr{F}^{\prime}_{ck}.

Finally, we set

\displaystyle g(1,h,k)

\displaystyle\coloneqq f_{\ref{obs_con_apex}}(h,k)\text{ and}

\displaystyle g(r,h,k)

\displaystyle\coloneqq h^{2}\cdot g(r-1,h,ck)+r^{3}k\cdot f_{\ref{obs_con_apex}}(h,k).

The function
g(r,h,k)
is a placeholder for the actual function
f_{\ref{apex_disc_obs}}
we wish to define to prove the assertion.
However, at the moment it is more convenient to make the dependency on the number of components of
Z
visible.
To obtain the result we wish for we may simply set
f_{\ref{apex_disc_obs}}(h,k)\coloneqq g(h,h,ck)
as
h
is always an upper bound on
r.

Our proof proceeds by induction on
r.

That is, we prove the following statement by induction on the number of components of
Z.

Inductive statement.

For every positive integer
k,
every graph
Z
on at most
h
vertices with at most
r
components, and every graph
G
one of the following hold

1.

G
contains
\mathfrak{F}_{k}
as a minor for some
\langle\mathfrak{F}_{r}\rangle_{r\in\mathbb{N}}\in\mathfrak{F}_{\mathsf{excl}(Z)},
or

2.

there exists a set
S\subseteq V(G)
of size at most
g(r,h,k)
such that
G-S\in\mathsf{excl}(Z).

Base of the induction.

The base follows in a straightforward way from
Lemma 12.3
.
This is true since for the base we may consider the case
r=1
where
g(r,h,k)=f_{\ref{obs_con_apex}}(h,k)
and
Z
is connected.
With
Z
being connected there is no difference between the connectivizations of
Z
and
Z
itself and therefore the claim follows.

Inductive step.

From here on, we may assume that
r\geq 2.

As a first step, we consider the set
\mathcal{Z}_{2}
of all
2
-connectivizations of
Z.

Notice that every graph
Z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}_{2}
has exactly
r-1
components.
Moreover,
|\mathcal{Z}_{2}|\leq|Z|^{2}\leq h^{2}.

For each
Z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}_{2}
we may now call upon the induction hypothesis and obtain either

•

a set
S_{Z^{\prime}}
of size at most
g(r-1,h,ck)
such that
G-S_{Z^{\prime}}\in\mathsf{excl}(Z^{\prime}),
or

•

\mathscr{F}^{\prime}_{ck}
as a minor of
G
for some
\langle\mathscr{F}^{\prime}_{t}\rangle_{t\in\mathbb{N}}\in\mathfrak{F}_{\mathsf{excl}(Z^{\prime})}.

Recall that, for every
\langle\mathscr{F}^{\prime}_{t}\rangle_{t\in\mathbb{N}}\in\mathfrak{F}_{{\sf excl}(Z^{\prime})}
there exists a
\langle\mathscr{F}_{t}\rangle_{t\in\mathbb{N}}\in\mathfrak{F}_{{\sf excl}(Z)}
such that
\mathscr{F}_{k}
is a minor of
\mathscr{F}^{\prime}_{ck}.

Thus, if for any
Z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}_{2}
we obtain the second outcome, we are immediately done.
Hence, we may assume to obtain the set
S_{Z^{\prime}}
as above for each
Z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}.

Let
S_{1}\coloneqq\bigcup_{Z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}_{2}}S_{Z^{\prime}}
and notice that
|S_{1}|\leq h^{2}\cdot g(r-1,h,ck).

Let
G_{1}\coloneqq G-S_{1}.

Observe that for every minimal subgraph
H
of
G
such that
H
contains
Z
as a minor it must hold, that any component of
G_{1}
may contain at most one component of
H.

Otherwise, there would exist components
H_{1}
and
H_{2}
of
H
such that there exists a path from
H_{1}
to
H_{2}
in
G_{1}
which avoids all other components of
H
in
G_{1}.

Such a path, however, would witness that
G_{1}
still contains a member of
\mathcal{Z}_{2}
as a minor.

For every
i\in[r]
let
\mathcal{K}_{i}
be the set of all components of
G_{1}
that contain the component
Z_{i}
of
Z
as a minor.
Moreover, for any set
\mathcal{K}
of components of
G_{1}
let
\overline{\mathcal{K}}
be the set of all components of
G_{1}
which do not belong to
\mathcal{K}.

We first distinguish two cases, one of which is easily dealt with, and then we enter, as part of the second case, an iterative construction process that eventually leads to either our obstruction or the desired
\mathsf{excl}(Z)
-modulator.
The cases we distinguish are

Case 1:

|\mathcal{K}_{i}|\geq r\cdot k
for every
i\in[r],
and

Case 2:

there exists
i_{1}\in[r]
such that
|\mathcal{K}_{i_{1}}|<r\cdot k.

Notice that, in
Case 1
, we may greedily select
k
components from
\mathcal{K}_{i}
for every
i
and thereby obtain
k\cdot Z
as a minor of
G_{1}.

Since
\langle t\cdot Z\rangle_{t\in\mathbb{N}}\in\mathfrak{F}_{\mathsf{excl}(Z)},
this outcome would complete our proof and thus, we may assume to be in
Case 2
.
In the following, we will, at times, apply a similar counting argument.
To facilitate our writing, we will simply refer to this argument as the
few components argument
.

We initialize
I_{1}\coloneq\{i_{1}\}
and iteratively construct sets
I_{q}
and
X_{q}
as follows.

Target of our construction.

For every
q\geq 1
there exists a set
I_{q}=\{i_{1},\dots,i_{q}\}\subseteq I
such that there exists a set
X_{q}
of size at most
qr\cdot rk\cdot f_{\ref{obs_con_apex}}(h,k)
and a set
\mathcal{Q}_{q}
of at most
q\cdot r\cdot k
components of
G_{1}
with the following properties

1.

for every
i\in I_{q}
and every component
K\in\mathcal{Q}_{q}
either
K-X_{q}
is
Z_{i}
-minor-free, or
K
contains
\mathscr{F}^{i}_{k}
as a minor for some
\langle\mathscr{F}^{i}_{t}\rangle_{t\in\mathbb{N}}\in\mathfrak{F}_{\mathsf{excl}(Z_{i})},

2.

for every
i\in I_{q}
and every component
K\in\overline{\mathcal{Q}_{q}}
it holds that
K
is
Z_{i}
-minor-free, and

3.

for every minimal subgraph
H
of
G_{1}-X_{q}
which contains
Z
as a minor and every
i\in I_{q}
there exists a component
K_{i}\in\mathcal{Q}_{q}
such that
H\cap K_{i}
is minimal with respect to containing
Z_{i}
as a minor.
Moreover, if
i,j\in I_{q}
are distinct, then so are
K_{i}
and
K_{j}.

Once we complete this construction, we obtain that
I_{r}=I.

During this construction, we stop whenever we reach a point where
G_{1}-X_{q}
is
Z
-minor-free, or contains our desired obstruction.
For the second outcome of this stopping condition we will employ a small argument based on Hall’s Theorem, this argument will finally also complete the case
q=r.

We proceed with this construction by induction on
q
and, as the base, show how to obtain the set
X_{1}.

Base of our construction.

We set
\mathcal{Q}_{1}\coloneqq\mathcal{K}_{i_{1}}.

Notice that the second and third conditions immediately hold by the definition of
\mathcal{K}_{i_{1}}
since no other component of
G_{1}
can contain
Z_{i_{1}}
as a minor and no
Z
-minor can realize two of its components in the same component of
G_{1}.

For each of the at most
r\cdot k
members of
\mathcal{K}_{i_{1}}
we may apply
Lemma 12.3
for
k
and the graph
Z_{i_{1}}.

Let
X_{1}
be the union of all hitting sets, of size at most
f_{\ref{obs_con_apex}}(h,k)
each, that are returned this way.
It follows that
I_{1}
and
X_{1}
also satisfy the first condition of our construction.

The inductive step of our construction.

Let us now assume that for some
q\in[r-1]
we have already constructed the sets
I_{q},

\mathcal{Q}_{q},
and
X_{q}
as above.

First, let us introduce the
matching argument
.
Let
B_{q}
be the bipartite graph with
I_{q}
as one of the two color classes and
\mathcal{Q}_{q}
as the other color class.
There exists an edge
iK\in E(B_{q})
if and only if the component
K\in\mathcal{Q}_{q}
still contains
Z_{i}
as a minor after deleting
X_{q}.

Notice that, by the second condition of our construction, this means that
K
must contain
\mathscr{F}^{i}_{k}
as a minor for some
\langle\mathscr{F}^{i}_{t}\rangle_{t\in\mathbb{N}}\in\mathfrak{F}_{\mathsf{excl}(Z_{i})}.

Now, if
B_{q}
does not have a matching that covers the entire set
I_{q},
then, by Hall’s Theorem, there exists a set
I^{\prime}\subseteq I_{q}
such that
|N_{B_{q}}(I^{\prime})|<|I^{\prime}|.

Since every occurrence of
Z
as a minor in
G_{1}-X_{q}
must use
q
distinct members of
\mathcal{Q}_{q},
one for each
i\in I_{q},
to realize the components
Z_{i},

i\in I_{q},
this implies that
G_{1}-X_{q}
is
Z
-minor-free and we may terminate.
Hence, we may assume that
{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}\mathcal{Q}_{q}
contains
\mathscr{F}_{k}
as a minor for some
\langle\mathscr{F}_{t}\rangle_{t\in\mathbb{N}}\in\mathfrak{F}_{\mathsf{excl(I_{q})}}.

Now suppose that
|\mathcal{K}_{i}\setminus\mathcal{Q}_{q}|\geq|I\setminus I_{q}|k
for every
i\in I\setminus I_{q}.

By the
few components argument
and the
matching argument
above this implies that
G_{1}
contains
\mathscr{F}_{k}
as a minor for some
\langle\mathscr{F}_{t}\rangle_{t\in\mathbb{N}}\in\mathfrak{F}_{\mathsf{excl}(Z)}.

Hence, we may assume that there is some
i_{q+1}\in I\setminus I_{q}
such that
|\mathcal{K}_{q+1}\setminus\mathcal{Q}_{q}|<|I\setminus I_{q}|k.

Let
\mathcal{Q}_{q+1}\coloneqq\mathcal{Q}_{q}\cup\mathcal{K}_{i_{q+1}}.

Next, we make use of
Lemma 12.3
.
That is, we apply
Lemma 12.3
for
Z_{i_{q+1}}
and every member of
\mathcal{Q}_{q+1}.

Let
X_{q+1}^{\prime}
be the union of all modulators, each of size at most
f_{\ref{obs_con_apex}}(h,k),
which are returned by these applications.
Notice that

\displaystyle|X_{q+1}^{\prime}|

\displaystyle\leq(q+1)\cdot rk\cdot f_{\ref{obs_con_apex}}(h,k).

Finally, let
X_{q+1}\coloneqq X_{q}\cup X_{q+1}^{\prime}
and notice that

\displaystyle|X_{q+1}|\leq|X_{q}|+(q+1)\cdot rk\cdot f_{\ref{obs_con_apex}}(h,k)\leq(q+1)r\cdot rk\cdot f_{\ref{obs_con_apex}}(h,k).

The three sets
I_{q+1},

\mathcal{Q}_{q+1},
and
X_{q+1}
now satisfy the first condition of our construction by
Lemma 12.3
and our construction of
X_{q+1},
and the second and third condition are satisfied by our choice for
\mathcal{Q}_{q+1}.

In particular, it follows from the
matching argument
that, if
\bigcup\mathcal{Q}_{q+1}
does not contain
\sum_{j\in[q+1]}\mathscr{F}^{j}_{k}
as a minor for some choice of
\langle\mathscr{F}^{j}_{t}\rangle_{t\in\mathbb{N}}\in\mathfrak{F}_{\mathsf{excl}(Z_{j})}
for each
j\in I_{q+1},
then
G_{1}-X_{q+1}
is
Z
-minor-free.
This last observation indeed proves that, once
I_{r},

\mathcal{Q}_{r},
and
X_{r}
are constructed, we are done.
∎

Concluding,
Corollary 12.7
and
Lemma 12.8
imply
that
\mathfrak{F}_{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathsf{min}}
is a universal obstruction for
{\sf apex}_{\mathcal{H}}.

12.3
Universal obstructions for
\mathcal{H}
-
{\sf td}

So far we presented universal obstructions for the
parameters
\mathcal{H}
-
{\sf tw}
and
{\sf apex}_{\mathcal{H}},
namely

\mathfrak{W}_{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathsf{min}}
and
\mathfrak{F}_{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathsf{min}}
respectively.
Notice that the knowledge of
\mathfrak{W}_{\mathcal{H}}
has been important
for the derivation of
\mathfrak{F}_{\mathcal{H}}
as it permitted to assume
that
\mathcal{H}
-
tw
is bounded and consider a bounded width
tree-
{\sf apex}_{\mathcal{H}}
decomposition in the proof of
Lemma 12.3
.
We next apply the same approach to the
elimination distance

to
\mathcal{H},
also known as the
\mathcal{H}
-tree-depth
,
that is recursively defined as follows:

\displaystyle{\sf td}_{\mathcal{H}}(G)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}0&\text{if}\ G\in\mathcal{H},\\
1+\min\{{\sf ed}_{\mathcal{H}}(G\setminus\{v\})\mid v\in V(G)\}&\text{if}\ G\text{ is connected},\\
\max\{{\sf ed}_{\mathcal{H}}(H)\mid H\text{ is a connected component of}\ G\}&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

(28)

Another way to define
{\sf td}_{\mathcal{H}}
is to just replace in
(
6
)
{\sf tw}
by
{\sf td},
that is the parameter of
tree-depth
.
The
tree-depth
of a graph
G
is
defined as the minimum height of a rooted forest
F

with the property that every edge of
G
connects a pair of vertices that have an ancestor-descendant relationship to each other in
F.

It is easy to observe that
{\sf td}={\sf td}_{\varnothing}
(recall that
\varnothing={\sf excl}(K_{1})
).
The parameter
{\sf td}_{\mathcal{H}}
was introduced by
Bulian and Dawar
[
9
,
10
]

as a distance measure of a graph to some graph class
\mathcal{H}.

Notice that
{\sf td}_{\mathcal{H}}
remains the same parameter if we replace
\mathcal{H}
by
\mathcal{H}^{(c)}.

It can be easily seen that for every graph
G,

{\sf tw}_{\mathcal{H}}(G)\leq\mathcal{H}
-
{\sf tw}(G)\leq{\sf apex}_{\mathcal{H}}(G).

For every
k,
let

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}\mathcal{D}_{k}^{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{G\mid\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf td}(G)\leq k\}.

(29)

In
[
10
]
, Bulian and Dawar proved that there is a computable function
bounding the obstructions of
\mathcal{T}\mathcal{D}_{k}^{\mathcal{H}},
i.e., for every
\mathcal{H},
there is a computable function
f_{\mathcal{H}}^{\sf td}:\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}
where
for every
k\in\mathbb{N},
if
Z\in{\sf obs}(\mathcal{T}\mathcal{D}_{k}^{\mathcal{H}}),
then
|Z|\leq f_{\mathcal{H}}^{\sf td}(k).
This implied that the problem of deciding whether
\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf td}(G)\leq k
is constructively in
FPT
, when parameterized by
k.
This was improved in
[
53
]
where a time
2^{2^{2^{\text{$\mathsf{poly}$}_{h}(k)}}}n^{2}
algorithm for this problem was given. Moreover, the results in
[
53
]
imply an explicit bound
f_{\mathcal{H}}^{\sf td}(k)=2^{2^{2^{2^{\text{$\mathsf{poly}$}_{h}(k)}}}}.

In this section, we explain how to use an approach similar to the one of
Subsection 12.1
in order to find a universal obstruction for
\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf td}.

We start from the following key observation of Bulian and Dawar in
[
10
]
.

Lemma 12.9
.

For every minor-closed class
\mathcal{H},

{\sf obs}(\mathcal{H}^{(c)})=\mathsf{conn}({\sf obs}(\mathcal{H})).

A
2-rooted-graph
is a triple
\mathbf{Ζ}=(Ζ,v,u)
where
Z
is a connected graph and
v,u\in V(Ζ).

We say that two 2-rooted-graphs
\mathbf{Ζ}=(Ζ,v,u)
and
\mathbf{Ζ^{\prime}}=(Ζ^{\prime},v^{\prime},u^{\prime})
are
isomorphic

if there is an isomorphism
\sigma:V(Ζ)\to V(Ζ^{\prime})
such that
\sigma(v)=v^{\prime}
and
\sigma(u)=u^{\prime}.

We define
\mathcal{R}(Ζ)
as the set of all pairwise non-isomorphic 2-rooted-graphs whose graph is
Ζ.
An
\mathcal{H}
-chain
of size
t
is a graph obtained
if we first consider a sequence

\displaystyle\langle(Ζ^{1},v^{1},u^{1}),\ldots,(Ζ^{t},v^{t},u^{t})\rangle

(30)

of
2
-rooted graphs, where for each
i\in[t],
there exists some
Z^{i}\in{\sf obs}(\mathcal{H})
such that
(Z^{i},v^{i},u^{i})\in\mathcal{R}(Z),

and then add the edges in
\big\{\{u^{i},v^{i+1}\}\mid i\in[t-1]\big\}
in the disjoint union of the graphs in (
30
). An
\mathcal{H}
-chain where

(Z^{i},v^{i},u^{i})
is isomorphic to some particular
\mathbf{Ζ},
then we call it the
\mathbf{Ζ}
-chain
.
For every 2-rooted graph
\mathbf{Ζ}=(Ζ,v,u)\in\mathcal{R}(Ζ),
we define
the parametric graph
\mathscr{E}^{\mathbf{Ζ}}=\langle\mathscr{E}^{\mathbf{Ζ}}_{t}\rangle_{t\in\mathbb{N}}

where
\mathscr{E}^{\mathbf{Ζ}}_{t}
is the
\mathbf{Z}
-chain of size
t.

Observation 12.10
.

There exists a function
f:\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}
such that
for every minor-closed class
\mathcal{H}

and every
k\in\mathbb{N},
every
\mathcal{H}
-chain of size
t\cdot f(h)

contains
\mathscr{E}^{\mathbf{Ζ}}_{t}
as a minor
for some
\mathbf{Ζ}\in\mathcal{R}(Ζ).

Let
\mathcal{H}
be a minor-closed class and let
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}}={\sf obs}(\mathcal{H}^{(c)}).

Then we define the following
collections of parametric graphs.

\displaystyle\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq

\displaystyle\mathfrak{W}_{\mathcal{H}}\cup\bigcup_{Z\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}}}\big\{\mathscr{E}^{\mathbf{Ζ}}\mid\mathbf{Ζ}\in\mathcal{R}(Ζ)\big\}

(31)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathsf{min}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq

\displaystyle\mathsf{min}(\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{H}})

The next observation follows immediately from (
28
)
and the definition of
\mathscr{E}^{\mathbf{Ζ}}.

Observation 12.11
.

Let
\mathcal{H}
be a minor-closed class and
Z\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}}.
Then for every graphs
G,
every 2-rooted-graph graph
\mathbf{Z}\in\mathcal{R}(Ζ),
every

\mathscr{E}^{\mathbf{Ζ}}=\langle\mathscr{E}^{\mathbf{Ζ}}_{t}\rangle_{{t\in\mathbb{N}}},
and every
t\in\mathbb{N},

it holds that

{\sf td}_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathscr{E}^{\mathbf{Ζ}}_{t})=\Omega(\log(t)).

Theorem 12.12
.

Let
\mathcal{H}
be a minor-closed class. Then

\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{H}}
is a universal obstruction for
\mathcal{H}
-
{\sf td}
with single exponential gap.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{E}_{\mathcal{H}}\coloneqq\bigcup_{Z\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}}}\big\{\mathscr{E}^{\mathbf{Ζ}}\mid\mathbf{Ζ}\in\mathcal{R}(Ζ)\big\}).

We first prove that
{\sf p}_{\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{H}}}\preceq\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf td}.
For this let

k\in\mathbb{N}
and let

\mathscr{T}_{k}\in\mathscr{T}=\langle\mathscr{T}_{k}\rangle_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\in\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{H}}.

In the case where
\mathscr{T}\in\mathfrak{W}_{\mathcal{H}}

we know from
Corollary 11.8
that
{\mathcal{H}}\text{-}{\sf td}(\mathscr{T}_{t})\geq{\mathcal{H}}\text{-}{\sf tw}(\mathscr{T}_{t})=\Omega_{h}(k),
where
h=h(\mathcal{H}).

In case where
\mathscr{T}\in\mathfrak{E}_{\mathcal{H}},
it follows from
Observation 12.11
that

{\mathcal{H}}\text{-}{\sf td}(\mathscr{G}_{t})=\Omega(\log(k)).
This proves
{\sf p}_{\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{H}}}\preceq\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf td}.

We proceed with the proof that
\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf td}\preceq{\sf p}_{\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{H}}}.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
and let

G
be a graph minor-excluding

\mathscr{T}_{k}\in\mathscr{T}
for every
\mathscr{T}\in\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{H}}.
W.l.o.g. we assume that
G
is connected, otherwise we apply the proof to each of the components of
G.

As
\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{H}}\lesssim^{*}\mathfrak{W}_{\mathcal{H}}

we know that
\mathcal{H}
-
{\sf tw}(G)\leq q\coloneqq{2^{k^{\mathcal{O}_{h}(1)}}}.

Let
(T,\beta)
be a
\mathcal{H}
-tree decomposition
of
G
of width
\leq q.

We define the parameter
\mathcal{H}
-
lc
(G)
as the maximum
k
for which

G
contains an
\mathcal{H}
-chain of size
k
as a minor. From
Observation 12.10

we have that
\mathcal{H}{\sf\text{-}lc}(G)

is bounded by
\mathcal{O}_{h}({\sf p}_{\mathfrak{E}_{\mathcal{H}}}(G))=\mathcal{O}_{h}(k).

Therefore, it is enough to prove that, for every pair of integers
k^{\prime},q^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N},

and every graph
G
of
\mathcal{H}
-treewidth at most
q^{\prime},
if
\mathcal{H}\mathsf{\text{-}lc}(G)\leq k^{\prime},
then
\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf td}(G)\leq(q^{\prime}+1)\cdot k^{\prime}.

For this, we use induction on
k^{\prime}.

For every edge
tt^{\prime}
of
T
we define
X_{t}
and
X_{t^{\prime}}
as in (
23
).

We first observe that if there is some edge
e=tt^{\prime}
of
T
where
\mathcal{H}\mathsf{\text{-}lc}(G[X_{t}\setminus X_{t^{\prime}}])\leq k^{\prime}-1
and
\mathcal{H}\mathsf{\text{-}lc}(G[X_{t^{\prime}}\setminus X_{t}])\leq k^{\prime}-1,
then

\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf td}(G)\leq|X_{t}\cap X_{t^{\prime}}|+\max\{\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf td}(G[X_{t}\setminus X_{t^{\prime}}]),\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf td}(G[X_{t^{\prime}}\setminus X_{t}])\}\leq k^{\prime}+(q^{\prime}+1)\cdot(k^{\prime}-1)=(q^{\prime}+1)\cdot k^{\prime}.

On the other side if it holds that if
\mathcal{H}\mathsf{\text{-}lc}(G[X_{t}\setminus X_{t^{\prime}}])=\mathcal{H}\mathsf{\text{-}lc}(G[X_{t^{\prime}}\setminus X_{t}])=k^{\prime},
then the connected graph
G
contains some subgraph
Z
of
G[X_{t}\setminus X_{t^{\prime}}]
and some subgraph
Z^{\prime}
of
G[X_{t^{\prime}}\setminus X_{t}]
such that both
Z
and
Z^{\prime}
are
\mathcal{H}
-chains of size
k^{\prime}
and there is a path joining
Z
and
Z^{\prime}.

This leads to a contradiction because two
\mathcal{H}
-chains of length
k^{\prime}
connected by some path implies the existence
of some
\mathcal{H}
-chain of length
k^{\prime}+1.

The conclusion of the above is that for every
e=tt^{\prime}
exactly one of
G[X_{t}\setminus X_{t^{\prime}}]
and
G[X_{t^{\prime}}\setminus X_{t}]
contains a
\mathcal{H}
-chain of length
k^{\prime}.

We orient every edge towards the direction where the
\mathcal{H}
-chain of length
k^{\prime}
is located.
This implies that there is a node
t
of
T
which is a sink with respect to this orientation of
T.

If
t
is not a leaf of
T
let
A\coloneqq\beta(t),
otherwise let
A\coloneqq A_{t}
where
A_{t}
is the unique adhesion set of the leaf
t\in V(T).

As a consequence, if
C
is a connected component of
G-A,
then
\mathcal{H}\mathsf{\text{-}lc}(C)\leq k^{\prime}-1,
therefore, by the induction hypothesis,
\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf td}(G)\leq(q^{\prime}+1)\cdot(k^{\prime}-1).

Then,

\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf td}(G)\leq|X_{t}|+\max\{\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf td}(C)\mid C\in\mathsf{cc}(G-A)\}\leq q^{\prime}+1+(q^{\prime}+1)\cdot(k^{\prime}-1)=(q^{\prime}+1)\cdot k^{\prime}

which completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

13
Consequences and open problems

In this paper we provided obstructing sets for the parameters
\mathcal{H}
-
{\sf tw},

\mathcal{H}
-
{\sf td},
and
{\sf apex}_{\mathcal{H}}.

Interestingly, our obstructing set
\mathfrak{W}_{\mathcal{H}}
for
\mathcal{H}
-
{\sf tw}
is a common ingredient to all other obstructing sets constructed in this paper.
Our proofs are all essentially based on the fact that excluding the parametric graphs in
\mathfrak{W}_{\mathcal{H}}
allows for the assumption of the existence of a
\mathcal{H}
-tree decomposition of bounded width.
This, in turn, facilitates the proofs that determine the rest of the parametric graphs in the obstructing sets for
\mathcal{H}
-
{\sf td},
and
{\sf apex}_{\mathcal{H}}.

In the case of
{\sf apex}_{\mathcal{H}},
the “half-integral” nature of the walloids in
\mathfrak{W}_{\mathcal{H}}
is the essential reason for the half-integral Erdős-Pósa property of graph minors as asserted by Liu’s theorem.
Moreover, this is exactly how we obtain our constructive proof of Thomas’ conjecture and thereby completely delineate the borderline between integrality and half-integrality in Erdős-Pósa dualities for minors.

The algorithmic consequences have already been discussed in
Subsection 1.3
. We discuss below further directions yet to be investigated.

Compositions of classes and parameter.

Let
\mathcal{H}
be a minor-closed graph class and let
{\sf p}
be a minor-monotone graph parameter.
Extending the idea of the definition in (
6
), we define the
composition
of
\mathcal{H}
and
{\sf p}
as the parameter
\mathcal{H}
-
{\sf p}\colon\mathcal{G}_{{\text{\rm{all}}}}\to\mathbb{N}
where

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf p}(G)=\min\{k\mid\penalty\

\displaystyle\text{there exists }X\subseteq V(G)\text{ s.{}t.{}\penalty\ }{\sf p}(\mathsf{torso}(G,X))\leq k\text{ and}

(32)

\displaystyle\text{every component of $G-X$ belongs to $\mathcal{H}$}\}

Notice that if
{\sf p}\preceq{\sf p}^{\prime}
then
\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf p}\preceq\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf p}^{\prime}.

From this point of view,
\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf tw}\preceq\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf td}\preceq\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf size}
is implied immediately by the fact that
{\sf tw}\preceq{\sf td}\preceq{\sf size}.

These three parameters have been studied extensively from the algorithmic point of view in
[
18
,
31
,
4
,
5
,
32
,
29
,
53
]
.

Observe that the edgeless graphs in
\langle t\cdot K_{1}\rangle_{t\in\mathbb{N}}
is a universal obstruction of
{\sf size}

and that the paths in
\langle P_{t}\rangle_{t\in\mathbb{N}}
is a universal obstruction of
{\sf td}.

According to (
22
), another way to define the collection
\mathfrak{S}_{\mathcal{H}}
is the following.

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{W}_{\mathcal{H}}\cup\bigcup_{Z\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}}}\big\{\langle t\cdot Z\rangle_{t\in\mathbb{N}}\big\}

(33)

Let’s discuss the similarities in the definitions of (
31
) and (
33
).
The first is that
\mathfrak{W}_{\mathcal{H}}
always appears as a common “half-integral” component.
Notice that, in (
31
),
\big\{\mathscr{E}^{\mathbf{Ζ}}\mid\mathbf{Ζ}\in\mathcal{R}(Ζ)\big\}
expresses all possible ways of substituting the vertices an edges in the paths of the universal obstructions of
{\sf td}
by replacing vertices and edges by an obstruction
Z
of
\mathcal{H}.

Similarly, in (
33
),
\langle t\cdot Z\rangle_{t\in\mathbb{N}}
substitutes the vertices of the edgeless graphs by the universal obstructions of
{\sf size}
by copies of some obstruction
Z
of
\mathcal{H}.

We believe that a similar composition mechanism should be able to construct universal obstructions for every parameter
\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf p},
where
{\sf tw}\preceq{\sf p}\preceq{\sf size}
and
{\sf p}\not\in\{{\sf tw},{\sf size}\}.

Assume that
{\sf p}
has some
finite
universal obstruction
\mathfrak{Q}
and that
\mathscr{Q}=\langle\mathscr{Q}_{t}\rangle_{t\in\mathbb{N}}
is one of its parametric graphs.
Given some obstruction
Z
of
\mathcal{H},
we apply the following transformation to each
\mathscr{Q}_{t}
: pick three
2
-rooted graphs
(Z^{v},v,x_{v}),(Z^{x},x,x),(Z^{u},x_{u},u)\in\mathcal{R}(Ζ)
and consider the 2-rooted graph
\overline{\mathbf{Z}}=(\overline{Z},v,u)
where
\overline{Z}
is obtained by the disjoint union of
Z^{v},

Z^{x},
and
Z^{u}
and the identification of
x,

x_{v},
and
x_{u}
to
x.

We denote by
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{Z}
the set containing all 2-rooted graphs
\overline{\mathbf{Z}}
that can be defined in this way.
Next, we replace each edge
\{v,u\}
of
\mathscr{Q}_{t}
by
\overline{\mathbf{Z}}=(\overline{Z},v,u)
in the natural way.
We denote by
\mathscr{Q}^{\overline{Z}}=\langle\mathscr{Q}_{t}^{\overline{Z}}\rangle_{t\in\mathbb{N}}
the parametric graph obtained this way.
We believe that this procedure creates an obstructing set for
\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf p}
from
\mathfrak{W}_{\mathcal{H}}
together with a
finite
collection of parametric graphs whose structure is “upper bounded” by some
\mathscr{Q}^{\overline{\mathbf{Z}}},
as above.
In particular, we set

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq

\displaystyle\mathsf{min}(\mathfrak{W}_{\mathcal{H}}\cup\bigcup_{Z\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}}}\big\{\mathscr{Q}^{\overline{\mathbf{Z}}}\mid\overline{\mathbf{Ζ}}\in\overline{\mathcal{R}}(Ζ)\big\})

and we conjecture that if
{\sf p}
has a finite universal obstruction
\mathfrak{Z}_{\mathcal{H}},
then

\mathfrak{Z}_{\mathcal{H}}\lesssim^{*}\mathfrak{G}_{\mathcal{H}}.

We stress that this conjecture, if resolved, would indicate that
\mathfrak{W}_{\mathcal{H}}
is “ubiquitous” to the universal obstructions of
\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf p}.

However, it would not give a finite universal obstruction to
\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf p}
but rather an “upper bound” revealing some of the structural characteristics of an obstruction set.
An interesting question is whether the result of Thomas in
[
78
]
on the BQO of graphs with respect to the minor relation may be useful for proving the finiteness of the obstructions of
\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf p}
for particular instantiations of
{\sf p}.

Bounding the size of the universal obstructions.

According to (
5
),
|\mathfrak{F}_{\mathcal{H}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}|\in 2^{2^{\mathcal{O}(\ell(h^{2}))}},
where
\ell(\cdot)
is the linkage function for which the best known bound is the one in
[
40
]
which is at least triple exponential.
This bound propagates to all obstructing sets defined in this paper, i.e.,
|\mathfrak{W}_{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathsf{min}}|,|\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathsf{min}}|,|\mathfrak{F}_{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathsf{min}}|,
and it would be nice to improve it.
Such an improvement would be useful for the actual construction of all embedding pairs that generate
\mathfrak{F}_{\mathcal{H}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}},
especially when
{\sf obs}(\mathcal{H})
becomes complicated.
We believe that much better upper bounds can be obtained at the price of a worse gap function.
This is because considering bigger walloids may provide us with enough space for proving that more complicated embedding pairs are “absorbed” by simpler ones which would allow us to discard some of the members of the obstructing set
\mathfrak{W}_{\mathcal{H}}
for
\mathcal{H}
-treewidth.

Our conjecture is that the bound
2^{2^{\mathcal{O}(\ell(h^{2}))}}
can be considerably improved to one that is polynomial in
h.

However, we do not believe that this may become possible without paying the price of a worse gap function in our parametric equivalences.

Only few obstructions are enough.

Let
\mathcal{O}=\langle\mathcal{O}_{k}\rangle_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
be some parametric family of antichains for the minor relation.
We say that
\mathcal{O}

defines
the parameter
{\sf p}
if
{\sf p}(G)
is the minimum
k
such that
G
excludes all graphs in
\mathcal{O}_{k}
as minors.
We would like to suggest the following interpretation of our obstructing sets (or better, the universal obstructions obtained from their minimizations) for the parameters
\mathcal{H}
-
tw
,
\mathcal{H}
-
td
, and
{\sf apex}_{\mathcal{H}}
:
We first define

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}\mathcal{W}_{k}^{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{G\mid\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf tw}(G)\leq k\},

(34)

and recall the analogous definitions of the graph classes
\mathcal{T}\mathcal{D}_{k}^{\mathcal{H}}
and
\mathcal{A}\mathcal{P}_{k}^{\mathcal{H}}
given in (
29
) and (
21
) respectively.
Using the terminology above, it holds that

\displaystyle{\sf p}_{{\sf obs}(\mathcal{T}\mathcal{W}_{k}^{\mathcal{H}})}=\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf tw},

\displaystyle{\sf p}_{{\sf obs}(\mathcal{T}\mathcal{D}_{k}^{\mathcal{H}})}=\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf td},\ \ \text{and}

\displaystyle{\sf p}_{{\sf obs}(\mathcal{A}\mathcal{P}_{k}^{\mathcal{H}})}={\sf apex}_{\mathcal{H}}.

Unfortunately, the sizes of the parametric families
{\sf obs}(\mathcal{T}\mathcal{W}_{k}^{\mathcal{H}}),

{\sf obs}(\mathcal{T}\mathcal{D}_{k}^{\mathcal{H}}),
and
{\sf obs}(\mathcal{A}\mathcal{P}_{k}^{\mathcal{H}})
grow fast as
k
increases.
For instance, in the base case where
\mathcal{H}=\varnothing,
where we obtain the parameters
{\sf tw}
/
{\sf td}
/
\mathsf{vc},
24
24

24

we use
\mathsf{vc}
for the vertex cover number of a graph.
exponential lower bounds to the number of obstructions are known, see
[
58
]
for
{\sf tw},

[
16
]
for
{\sf td},
and
[
14
]
for
\mathsf{vc}.

Also, as we already mentioned in the beginnings of
Section 12
and
Subsection 12.3
there are explicit upper bounds for the sizes of
{\sf obs}(\mathcal{T}\mathcal{D}_{k}^{\mathcal{H}})
and
{\sf obs}(\mathcal{A}\mathcal{P}_{k}^{\mathcal{H}})
(to our knowledge no explicit bound to the size of the members of
{\sf obs}(\mathcal{T}\mathcal{W}_{k}^{\mathcal{H}})
is known).
Given the current state of the art, it appears rather hopeless to expect that some precise characterization of these obstruction sets may be found.
Our obstructing sets suggest that this becomes possible if we drop our expectations and only ask for parametric equivalence. In fact, our results imply that,
no matter the value of
k
, we may discard all but
2^{2^{\mathcal{O}(\ell(h^{2}))}}
of the members of
{\sf obs}(\mathcal{T}\mathcal{W}_{k}^{\mathcal{H}})
/
{\sf obs}(\mathcal{T}\mathcal{D}_{k}^{\mathcal{H}})
/
{\sf obs}(\mathcal{A}\mathcal{P}_{k}^{\mathcal{H}})
and still have the following parametric families of antichains

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf tw}}=\langle\mathcal{O}_{k}^{{\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf tw}}}\rangle_{k\in\mathbb{N}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf td}}=\langle\mathcal{O}_{k}^{{\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf td}}}\rangle_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\ \ \text{and}

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{{\sf apex}_{\mathcal{H}}}=\langle\mathcal{O}_{k}^{{\sf apex}_{\mathcal{H}}}\rangle_{k\in\mathbb{N}},

where the parameters defined by these sets are equivalent to
\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf tw},

\mathcal{H}\text{-}{\sf td},
and
{\sf apex}_{\mathcal{H}}.

This means that “only”
2^{2^{\mathcal{O}(\ell(h^{2}))}}
obstructions are enough for determining the parametric behavior of
\mathcal{H}\text{-}\mathsf{tw},

\mathcal{H}\text{-}\mathsf{td},
and
{\sf apex}_{\mathcal{H}},
for
any
choice of the class
\mathcal{H}.

An interesting question is whether by including more (but still not too many) obstructions in the obstruction sets above one can obtain better parametric (preferably with polynomial or even linear gaps) estimations for the corresponding parameters.
Is there an interpretation of the constant-factor approximation algorithms in
[
32
]
via parametric graph
exclusion?

Strict and strong brambles.

As we have seen in
Corollary 11.9
, for every minor-closed class
\mathcal{H},
the existence of a strict
\mathcal{H}
-bramble of order
f_{\ref{cor_bramble_to_half_int_bramble}}(h,k)
in a graph implies the existence of a
\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}
-
\mathcal{H}
-bramble of order
k.

The function
f_{\ref{cor_bramble_to_half_int_bramble}}(h,k)
here again depends on our general gap function and is single exponential in
k.

We would certainly like to improve this and we believe that it is indeed possible.
The reason is that the
\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}
-
\mathcal{H}
-brambles found by our proof are of very particular nature:
they stem directly from our walloids and their construction requires the full machinery of our proof and the supporting results from
[
39
]
and
[
77
]
.
However,
\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}
-
\mathcal{H}
-brambles may not only be certified by walloids and we cannot exclude the existence of better bounds.
For instance, when
\mathcal{H}
has some planar obstruction, that is when
{\mathcal{H}}
-
{\sf tw}={\sf tw}+\Theta_{h}(1),
much better dependencies are known.
In particular, Hatzel, Komosa, Pilipczuk, and Sorge proved in
[
28
]
that the existence of a
\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}
-bramble of order
k
is implied by a bramble of order
\Omega(k^{2}\cdot\mathsf{polylog}(k))
(see also
[
27
,
44
]
for related results).
Whether these bounds can be extended for other choices of
\mathcal{H}
is an open question.
For this, even a polynomial gap for the case where
\mathcal{H}
is the class of planar graphs would be interesting.
Another direction is to pursue better dependencies by relaxing the half-integrality, for instance by asking for a
\nicefrac{{1}}{{3}}
-
\mathcal{H}
-bramble or a
\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}
-
\mathcal{H}
-bramble where no vertex is used by more than
3
or
4
bramble elements.
Is there such a relaxation that may lead to a polynomial dependency for all
\mathcal{H}
s?

Special families of
\mathcal{H}
’s.

Our work leaves the project of precisely determining
\mathfrak{W}_{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathsf{min}}
for particular instantiations of the class
\mathcal{H}
as an open problem.
Are there additional properties for
\mathcal{H}
that may facilitate such a description?
As a first step in this direction, in
[
56
]
, we focus on a particular family of
\mathcal{H}
’s, namely those whose obstructions are all
Kuratowski connected
and which contain at least one obstruction that is a minor of a
shallow-vortex grid
.
Providing the definitions of these conditions escape the purposes of this paper (for the interested reader, the definitions can be found in
[
70
]
and
[
76
]
).
We wish to mention that when these conditions are imposed on
\mathcal{H},
the obstructing sets are much simpler:
They are one or two walloids capturing surfaces (that is, these walloids do not have flower segments) and, using our terminology, are those which are generated by pairs
(\Sigma,\mathbf{B})
where
\mathbf{B}=\emptyset,
and a third type of walloid which is generated by pairs of the form
(\Sigma^{(0,0)},\mathbf{B}^{*})
where
\mathbf{B}^{*}=\{\langle Z,\langle y\rangle\rangle\},

Z\in\mathsf{obs}(\mathcal{H}),
and
y
is an arbitrary vertex of
Z.

Panos Rondogiannis
for his pivotal influence, which contributed to the realization of this project.
